### 1800s to early 1900s

In New York City, while the German-Jewish community was well established 'uptown', the more numerous Jews who migrated from Eastern Europe faced tension 'downtown' with Irish and German Catholic neighbors, especially the Irish Catholics who controlled Democratic Party politics[^cite_note-174] [at the time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tammany_Hall). Jews successfully established themselves in the garment trades and in the needle unions in New York. By the 1930s they were a major political factor in New York, with strong support for the most liberal programs of the [New Deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Deal). They continued as a major element of the [New Deal Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Deal_Coalition), giving special support to the [Civil Rights Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Rights_Movement). By the mid-1960s, however, the [Black Power movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Power_movement) caused a growing separation between blacks and Jews, though both groups remained solidly in the Democratic camp.[^cite_note-175]

Earlier Jewish immigrants from Germany tended to be politically [conservative](6675.md), though many, including the devoutly Republican [Louis Marshall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Marshall), believed that Jews should not have a political direction at all.[^cite_note-176] Meanwhile, the wave of Jews from Eastern Europe starting in the early 1880s were generally more liberal or [left-wing](18499.md) and became the political majority.[^cite_note-Diner-2004-177] Many came to America with experience in the socialist, [anarchist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Anarchism) and communist movements as well as the [Labor Bund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Jewish_Labor_Union), emanating from Eastern Europe. Many Jews rose to leadership positions in the early 20th century [American labor movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_labor_movement) and helped to found unions that played a major role in left-wing politics and, after 1936, in [Democratic Party](5043544.md) politics.[^cite_note-Diner-2004-177]

American Jews generally leaned towards the Republican party in the second half of the 19th century and the early years of the 20th century. This was compounded by the strong [Evangelical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) imagery used by [William Jennings Bryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Jennings_Bryan) and other Populist Democrats, which caused fears of Antisemitism among American Jews. Additionally Republican candidates such as [William McKinley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_McKinley) and [Theodore Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodore_Roosevelt) were extremely popular with Jewish voters.[^cite_note-178] However, the majority of Jews have generally voted Democratic since at least 1916, when they voted 55% for [Woodrow Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodrow_Wilson), and especially since 1928, when 72% supported the unsuccessful candidacy of [Al Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Smith).[^cite_note-JVLvote-170]

With the election of [Franklin D. Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt), American Jews voted more solidly Democratic. They voted 90% for Roosevelt in the elections of 1940, and 1944, representing the highest of support, equaled only once since. In the election of 1948, Jewish support for Democrat [Harry S. Truman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_S._Truman) dropped to 75%, with 15% supporting the new [Progressive Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_Party_\(United_States,_1948\)).[^cite_note-JVLvote-170] As a result of lobbying, and hoping to better compete for the Jewish vote, both major party platforms had included a pro-Zionist plank since 1944,[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180] and supported the creation of a Jewish state; it had little apparent effect however, with 90% still voting other-than-Republican. In every election since, except for 1980, no Democratic presidential candidate has won with less than 67% of the Jewish vote. (In 1980, Carter obtained 45% of the Jewish vote. See below.)

### 1950s to 1990s

During the 1952 and 1956 elections, Jewish voters cast 60% or more of their votes for Democrat [Adlai Stevenson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adlai_Stevenson_II), while [General Eisenhower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwight_Eisenhower) garnered 40% of the Jewish vote for his reelection, the best showing to date for the Republicans since [Warren G. Harding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warren_G._Harding)'s 43% in 1920.[^cite_note-JVLvote-170] In 1960, 83% voted for Democrat [John F. Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy) against [Richard Nixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Nixon), and in 1964, 90% of American Jews voted for [Lyndon Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyndon_Johnson), over his Republican opponent, arch-conservative [Barry Goldwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Goldwater). [Hubert Humphrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Humphrey) garnered 81% of the Jewish vote in the 1968 elections in his losing bid for president against [Richard Nixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Nixon).[^cite_note-JVLvote-170]

During the Nixon re-election campaign of 1972, Jewish voters were apprehensive about [George McGovern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_McGovern) and only favored the Democrat by 65%, while Nixon more than doubled Republican Jewish support to 35%. In the election of 1976, Jewish voters supported Democrat [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter) by 71% over incumbent president [Gerald Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerald_Ford)'s 27%, but during the Carter re-election campaign of 1980, Jewish voters greatly abandoned the Democrat, with only 45% support, while Republican winner [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) garnered 39%, and 14% went to independent (former Republican) [John Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_B._Anderson).[^cite_note-JVLvote-170][^cite_note-Helmreich-2008-181]

During the Reagan re-election campaign of 1984, the Republican retained 31% of the Jewish vote, while 67% voted for Democrat [Walter Mondale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Mondale). The 1988 election saw Jewish voters favor Democrat [Michael Dukakis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Dukakis) by 64%, while [George H. W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_H._W._Bush) polled a respectable 35%, but during Bush's re-election attempt in 1992, his Jewish support dropped to just 11%, with 80% voting for [Bill Clinton](3356.md) and 9% going to independent [Ross Perot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ross_Perot). Clinton's re-election campaign in 1996 maintained high Jewish support at 78%, with 16% supporting [Bob Dole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Dole) and 3% for Perot.[^cite_note-JVLvote-170][^cite_note-Helmreich-2008-181]

### 2000s

In the [2000 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_United_States_presidential_election), [Joe Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Lieberman) became the first American Jew to run for national office on a major-party ticket when he was chosen as Democratic presidential candidate [Al Gore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Gore)'s vice-presidential nominee. The elections of 2000 and 2004 saw continued Jewish support for Democrats [Al Gore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Gore) and [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry), a Catholic, remain in the high- to mid-70% range, while Republican [George W. Bush](3414021.md)'s re-election in 2004 saw Jewish support rise from 19% to 24%.[^cite_note-Helmreich-2008-181][^cite_note-182]

In the [2008 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_United_States_presidential_election), 78% of Jews voted for [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama), who became the first [African American](2154.md) to be elected president.[^cite_note-183] Additionally, 83% of white Jews voted for Obama compared to just 34% of white Protestants and 47% of white Catholics, though 67% of those identifying with another religion and 71% identifying with no religion also voted for Obama.[^cite_note-184]

In the February [2016 New Hampshire Democratic Primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_presidential_primaries,_2016), [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) became the first Jewish candidate to win a state's presidential primary election.[^cite_note-185]

For congressional and senate races, since 1968, American Jews have voted about 70–80% for Democrats;[^cite_note-186] this support increased to 87% for Democratic House candidates during the 2006 elections.[^cite_note-187]



[David Levy Yulee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Levy_Yulee)

The first American Jew to serve in the Senate was [David Levy Yulee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Levy_Yulee), who was Florida's first Senator, serving 1845–1851 and again 1855–1861.

There were 19 Jews among the 435 US Representatives at the start of the [112th Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/112th_United_States_Congress);[^cite_note-188] 26 Democrats and 1 ([Eric Cantor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Cantor)) Republican. While many of these Members represented coastal cities and suburbs with significant Jewish populations, others did not (for instance, [Kim Schrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Schrier) of Seattle, Washington; [John Yarmuth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Yarmuth) of Louisville, Kentucky; and [David Kustoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Kustoff) and [Steve Cohen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Cohen_\(politician\)) of Memphis, Tennessee). The total number of Jews serving in the House of Representatives declined from 31 in the [111th Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/111th_United_States_Congress).[^cite_note-189] [John Adler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Adler) of New Jersey, [Steve Kagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Kagan) of Wisconsin, [Alan Grayson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Grayson) of Florida, and [Ron Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Klein) of Florida all lost their re-election bids, [Rahm Emanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahm_Emanuel) resigned to become the President's Chief of Staff; and [Paul Hodes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Hodes) of New Hampshire did not run for re-election but instead (unsuccessfully) sought his state's open Senate seat. [David Cicilline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Cicilline) of Rhode Island was the only Jewish American who was newly elected to the 112th Congress; he had been the Mayor of [Providence](19356538.md). The number declined when [Jane Harman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jane_Harman), [Anthony Weiner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Weiner), and [Gabby Giffords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabby_Giffords) resigned during the 112th Congress.

As of January 2014, there were five openly gay men serving in Congress, and two are Jewish: [Jared Polis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jared_Polis) of Colorado and [David Cicilline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Cicilline) of Rhode Island.

In November 2008, Cantor was elected as the [House Minority Whip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_whips_of_the_United_States_House_of_Representatives), the first Jewish Republican to be selected for the position.[^cite_note-190] In 2011, he became the first Jewish [House Majority Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Majority_Leader). He served as Majority Leader until 2014, when he resigned shortly after his loss in the Republican primary election for his House seat.

In 2013, Pew found that 70% of American Jews identified with or leaned toward the [Democratic Party](5043544.md), with just 22% identifying with or leaning toward the [Republican Party](32070.md).[^cite_note-191]

The [114th Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/114th_United_States_Congress) included 10 Jews[^cite_note-192] among 100 [U.S. Senators](24909346.md): nine Democrats ([Michael Bennet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Bennet), [Richard Blumenthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Blumenthal), [Brian Schatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Schatz), [Benjamin Cardin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Cardin), [Dianne Feinstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dianne_Feinstein), [Jon Ossoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Ossoff), [Jacky Rosen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacky_Rosen), [Charles Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Schumer), [Ron Wyden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Wyden)), and [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders), who became a Democrat to [run for President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders_2016_presidential_campaign) but returned to the Senate as an Independent.[^cite_note-193]

In the [118th Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/118th_United_States_Congress), there are 28 Jewish US Representatives.[^cite_note-194] 25 are Democrats and the other 3 are Republicans. All 10 Jewish Senators are Democrats.[^cite_note-195]

Additionally, six members of President [Joe Biden](145422.md)'s cabinet were Jewish ([Secretary of State](32293.md) [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken), [Attorney General](44002.md) [Merrick Garland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merrick_Garland), [DNI](456082.md) [Avril Haines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avril_Haines), [White House Chief of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House_Chief_of_Staff) [Ron Klain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Klain), [Homeland Security Secretary](212926.md) [Alejandro Mayorkas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Mayorkas), and [Treasury Secretary](43338.md) [Janet Yellen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janet_Yellen)).[^cite_note-196]

## Contemporary politics

Main article: [American Jews in politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Jews_in_politics)



Map of Jewish senators as of 2025. Blue indicates there is currently at least one Jewish senator from that state. Gray indicates no Jewish senators from that state.

American Jews are a distinctive and influential group in the nation's politics. Jeffrey S. Helmreich writes that the ability of American Jews to effect this through political or financial clout is overestimated,[^cite_note-197] that the primary influence lies in the group's voting patterns.[^cite_note-Helmreich-2008-181]

"Jews have devoted themselves to politics with almost religious fervor," writes [Mitchell Bard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitchell_Bard), who adds that Jews have the highest percentage [voter turnout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_turnout) of any ethnic group (84% reported being registered to vote[^cite_note-198]).

Though the majority (60–70%) of the country's Jews identify as Democratic, Jews span the political spectrum, with those at higher levels of observance being far more likely to vote Republican than their less observant and secular counterparts.[^cite_note-199]

Dr. Eric Uslaner of the [University of Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Maryland) argues, with regard to the 2004 election: "Only 15% of Jews said that Israel was a key voting issue. Among those voters, 55% voted for Kerry (compared to 83% of Jewish voters not concerned with Israel)." Uslander goes on to point out that negative views of [Evangelical Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) had a distinctly negative impact for Republicans among Jewish voters, while [Orthodox Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodox_Judaism), traditionally more conservative in outlook as to social issues, favored the Republican Party.[^cite_note-200] A *New York Times* article suggests that the Jewish movement to the Republican party is focused heavily on faith-based issues, similar to the Catholic vote, which is credited for helping [President Bush](3414021.md) taking Florida in 2004.[^cite_note-201] However, Natan Guttman, *[The Forward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Forward)*'s Washington bureau chief, dismisses this notion, writing in *[Moment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moment_\(magazine\))* that while "\[i]t is true that Republicans are making small and steady strides into the Jewish community ... a look at the past three decades of [exit polls](665947.md), which are more reliable than pre-election polls, and the numbers are clear: Jews vote overwhelmingly Democratic,"[^cite_note-202] an assertion confirmed by the most recent presidential election results.



[Florence Kahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence_Prag_Kahn) was the first Jewish woman to be elected to the [United States Congress](31756.md) and first woman to be reelected.

Owing to high Democratic identification in the [2008 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_United_States_presidential_election), 78% of Jews voted for Democrat [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) versus 21% for Republican [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain), despite Republican attempts to connect Obama to Muslim and pro-Palestinian causes.[^cite_note-Benhorin-2008-203] It has been suggested that running mate [Sarah Palin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Palin)'s conservative views on social issues may have nudged Jews away from the McCain–Palin ticket.[^cite_note-Helmreich-2008-181][^cite_note-Benhorin-2008-203] In the [2012 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_presidential_election), 69% of Jews voted for the Democratic incumbent President Obama.[^cite_note-204]

In 2019, after the 2016 election of [Donald Trump](4848272.md), poll data from the Jewish Electorate Institute showed that 73% of Jewish voters felt less secure as Jews than before, 71% disapproved of Trump's handling of antisemitism (54% strongly disapprove), 59% felt that he bears "at least some responsibility" for the [Pittsburgh synagogue shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsburgh_synagogue_shooting) and [Poway synagogue shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poway_synagogue_shooting), and 38% were concerned that Trump was encouraging right-wing extremism. Views of the Democratic and Republican parties were milder: 28% were concerned that Republicans were making alliances with white nationalists and tolerating antisemitism within their ranks, while 27% were concerned that Democrats were tolerating antisemitism within their ranks.[^cite_note-205]

In the [2020 U.S. Presidential Election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_U.S._Presidential_Election), 77% of American Jews voted for Joe Biden, while 22% voted for Donald Trump.[^cite_note-206] A similar trend emerged in the [2024 Presidential Election](59568546.md), where 78% of American Jews voted for [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) while 22% voted for Donald Trump.[^cite_note-207]

In September 2026, a national poll of Jewish voters ahead of the [2026 midterms](70357296.md) found that 67% of respondents planned to support a Democrat compared to 26% for a Republican in Congressional elections. Regarding approval of the [second Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_administration_of_Donald_Trump), 25% approved while 75% disapproved. The top priority issues were cost of living and affordability (37%), future of democracy (36%), and antisemitism (25%).[^cite_note-208]

### Domestic issues

Further information: [Jewish views on homosexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_views_on_homosexuality), [Judaism and abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism_and_abortion), and [Judaism and environmentalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism_and_environmentalism)

A 2013 Pew Research Center survey suggests that American Jews' views on domestic politics are intertwined with the community's self-definition as a persecuted minority who benefited from the liberties and societal shifts in the United States and feel obligated to help other minorities enjoy the same benefits. American Jews across age and gender lines tend to vote for and support politicians and policies which are supported by the [Democratic Party](5043544.md). On the other hand, Orthodox American Jews have domestic political views which are more similar to those of their religious Christian neighbors.[^cite_note-209]

American Jews are largely supportive of [LGBT rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights) with 79% responding in a 2011 Pew poll that homosexuality should be "accepted by society", while the overall average in the same 2011 poll among Americans of all demographic groups was that 50%.[^cite_note-210] A split on homosexuality exists by level of observance. [Reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Judaism) rabbis in America perform same-sex marriages as a matter of routine, and there are fifteen LGBT Jewish congregations in North America.[^cite_note-211] Reform, [Reconstructionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstructionist_Judaism) and, increasingly, [Conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Judaism), Jews are far more supportive on issues like gay marriage than [Orthodox Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodox_Judaism) are.[^cite_note-212] A 2007 survey of Conservative Jewish leaders and activists showed that an overwhelming majority supported gay rabbinical ordination and same-sex marriage.[^cite_note-213] Accordingly, 78% of Jewish voters rejected [Prop 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Proposition_8_\(2008\)), the bill that banned gay marriage in California. No other ethnic or religious group voted as strongly against it.[^cite_note-214]

A 2014 Pew poll found that American Jews mostly support abortion rights, with 83% answering that abortion should be legal in all or most cases.[^cite_note-215]

In considering the trade-off between the economy and environmental protection, American Jews were significantly more likely than other religious groups (excepting Buddhism) to favor stronger environmental protection.[^cite_note-216]

Jews in America also overwhelmingly oppose current United States marijuana policy. In 2009, eighty-six percent of Jewish Americans opposed arresting nonviolent marijuana smokers, compared to 61% for the population at large and 68% of all Democrats. Additionally, 85% of Jews in the United States opposed using federal law enforcement to close patient cooperatives for medical marijuana in states where medical marijuana is legal, compared to 67% of the population at large and 73% of Democrats.[^cite_note-217]

A 2014 Pew Research survey titled "How Americans Feel About Religious Groups", found that Jews were viewed the most favorably of all other groups, with a rating of 63 out of 100.[^cite_note-218] Jews were viewed most positively by fellow Jews, followed by white Evangelicals. Sixty percent of the 3,200 persons surveyed said they had ever met a Jew.[^cite_note-219]

### Foreign policy



[Winston Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Churchill) and [Bernard Baruch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Baruch) converse in the back seat of a car in front of Baruch's home.

American Jews have displayed a very strong interest in [foreign affairs](233404.md), especially regarding [Germany](11867.md) in the 1930s, and [Israel](9282173.md) since 1945.[^cite_note-220]

#### Zionism and Israel

[Zionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism) became a well-organized movement in the U.S. with the involvement of leaders such as [Louis Brandeis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Brandeis) and the promise of a reconstituted homeland in the [Balfour Declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balfour_Declaration).[^cite_note-221] Jewish Americans organized large-scale boycotts of German merchandise during the 1930s to protest [Nazi Germany](21212.md). [Franklin D. Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt)'s leftist domestic policies received strong Jewish support in the 1930s and 1940s, as did his anti-Nazi foreign policy and his promotion of the [United Nations](31769.md). Support for political Zionism in this period, although growing in influence, remained a distinctly minority opinion among Jews in the United States until about 1944–45, when the early rumors and reports of the systematic mass murder of the Jews in [Nazi-occupied countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi-occupied_countries) became publicly known with the liberation of the [Nazi concentration camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_concentration_camps) and [extermination camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extermination_camp). The founding of the modern State of [Israel](9282173.md) in 1948 and recognition thereof by the American government[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] (following objections by American isolationists) was an indication of both its intrinsic support and its response to learning the horrors of the Holocaust.

This attention was based on a natural affinity toward and support for Israel in the Jewish community. The attention is also because of the ensuing and unresolved conflicts regarding the founding of Israel and the role for the Zionist movement going forward. A lively internal debate commenced, following the [Six-Day War](29328.md). The American Jewish community was divided over whether or not they agreed with the Israeli response; the great majority came to accept the war as necessary.[^cite_note-224] Similar tensions were aroused by the 1977 election of [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin) and the rise of [Revisionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revisionist_Zionism) policies, the [1982 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War) and the continuing administrative governance of portions of the [West Bank](33209.md) territory.[^cite_note-225] Disagreement over Israel's 1993 acceptance of the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords) caused a further split among American Jews;[^cite_note-Seliktar-2007-226] this mirrored a similar split among Israelis and led to a parallel rift within the [pro-Israel lobby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_lobby_in_the_United_States), and even ultimately to the United States for its "blind" support of Israel.[^cite_note-Seliktar-2007-226] Abandoning any pretense of unity, both segments began to develop separate advocacy and lobbying organizations. The liberal supporters of the [Oslo Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accord) worked through [Americans for Peace Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Americans_for_Peace_Now) (APN), [Israel Policy Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Policy_Forum) (IPF) and other groups friendly to the Labour government in Israel. They tried to assure Congress that American Jewry was behind the Accord and defended the efforts of the administration to help the fledgling [Palestinian Authority](24093.md) (PA), including promises of financial aid. In a battle for public opinion, IPF commissioned a number of polls showing widespread support for Oslo among the community.

In opposition to Oslo, an alliance of conservative groups, such as the [Zionist Organization of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist_Organization_of_America) (ZOA), [Americans For a Safe Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Americans_For_a_Safe_Israel?action=edit\&redlink=1) (AFSI), and the [Jewish Institute for National Security Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Institute_for_National_Security_Affairs) (JINSA) tried to counterbalance the power of the liberal Jews. On October 10, 1993, the opponents of the Palestinian-Israeli accord organized at the [American Leadership Conference for a Safe Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Leadership_Conference_for_a_Safe_Israel?action=edit\&redlink=1), where they warned that Israel was prostrating itself before "an armed thug", and predicted and that the "thirteenth of September is a date that will live in infamy". Some Zionists also criticized, often in harsh language, Prime Minister [Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin) and [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres), his foreign minister and chief architect of the peace accord. With the community so strongly divided, AIPAC and the Presidents Conference, which was tasked with representing the national Jewish consensus, struggled to keep the increasingly antagonistic discourse civil. Reflecting these tensions, [Abraham Foxman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Foxman) from the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League) was asked by the conference to apologize for criticizing ZOA's [Morton Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morton_Klein). The conference, which under its organizational guidelines was in charge of moderating communal discourse, reluctantly censured some Orthodox spokespeople for attacking [Colette Avital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colette_Avital), the Labor-appointed Israeli [Consul General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consul_General) in New York and an ardent supporter of that version of a peace process.[^cite_note-227]

Since the outbreak of the [Gaza war](74998519.md), views on Zionism and Israel have shifted among American Jews. In a March 2025 poll from the [Jewish Federations of North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Federations_of_North_America) among American Jews, 71% reported emotional attachment to Israel, 60% says Israel makes them proud to be Jewish, 88% supported Israel as a [Jewish and democratic state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_and_democratic_state), and 37% self-identified as Zionist.[^cite_note-228]

#### Iraq war

Jewish Americans were more strongly opposed to the [Iraq War](5043324.md) than any other ethnic group, or even most Americans. The greater opposition to the war was not simply a result of high Democratic identification among Jewish Americans, as Jewish Americans of all political persuasions were more likely to oppose the war than non-Jews who shared the same political leanings.[^cite_note-229]

## Discrimination

See also: [Antisemitism in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_the_United_States)

[Hate crimes](13547.md) against Jews perpetrated by white supremacists have been increasing due to [white supremacy in the United States](41154041.md). Jews are more than twice as likely to be victims of hate crimes than [black](2154.md) or [Muslim Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-230]

## Jewish American culture

See also: [Jewish secularism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_secularism) and [Jewish culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_culture)

Since the time of the last major wave of Jewish immigration to America (over 2,000,000 Jews from Eastern Europe who arrived between 1890 and 1924), Jewish secular culture in the United States has become integrated in almost every important way with the broader American culture. Many aspects of Jewish American culture have, in turn, become part of the wider culture of the United States.

### Language

Most American Jews today are native [English speakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language). A variety of other languages are still spoken within some American Jewish communities that are representative of the various [Jewish ethnic divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_ethnic_divisions) from around the world that have come together to make up all of America's Jewish population.

Many of America's [Hasidic Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasidic_Judaism), being exclusively of [Ashkenazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkenazi_Jews) descent, are raised speaking [Yiddish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yiddish_language). Yiddish was once spoken as the primary language by most of the several million Ashkenazi Jews who migrated to the United States. It was, in fact, the original language in which *[The Forward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Forward)* was published. Yiddish has had an influence on [American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_English), and words borrowed from it include *[chutzpah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chutzpah)* ('effrontery, gall'), *nosh* ('snack'), *schlep* ('drag'), *schmuck* ('an obnoxious, contemptible person', euphemism for 'penis'), and, depending on [idiolect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idiolect), hundreds of other terms. (See also [Yinglish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yinglish).)

Many [Mizrahi Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mizrahi_Jews), including those from Arab countries such as Syria, Egypt, Iraq, Yemen, Morocco, Libya, etc. speak [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic). There are communities of Mizrahim in [Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn). The town of [Deal, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deal,_New_Jersey), is notably mostly Syrian-Jewish, with many of them Orthodox.[^cite_note-235]

The [Persian Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_Jews) community in the United States, notably the large community in and around Los Angeles and [Beverly Hills, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly_Hills,_California), primarily speak [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language) (see also [Judeo-Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dzhidi_language)) in the home and synagogue. They also support their own Persian language newspapers. Persian Jews also reside in eastern parts of New York such as [Kew Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kew_Gardens,_Queens) and [Great Neck, Long Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Neck,_New_York).

Many recent Jewish immigrants from the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) speak primarily Russian at home, and there are several notable communities where public life and business are carried out mainly in Russian, such as in [Brighton Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brighton_Beach) in New York City and [Sunny Isles Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunny_Isles_Beach) in Florida. 2010 estimates of the number of Jewish Russian-speaking households in the New York city area are around 92,000, and the number of individuals are somewhere between 223,000 and 350,000.[^cite_note-236] Another high population of Russian Jews can be found in the [Richmond District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_District,_San_Francisco,_California) of San Francisco where Russian markets stand alongside the numerous Asian businesses.



A typical poster-hung wall in Jewish Brooklyn, New York

American [Bukharan Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bukharan_Jews) speak [Bukhori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bukhori), a dialect of Tajik Persian. They publish their own newspapers such as the *Bukharian Times* and a large portion live in [Queens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queens), New York. [Forest Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Hills,_Queens,_New_York) in the New York City borough of Queens is home to 108th Street, which is called by some "Bukharian Broadway",[^cite_note-237] a reference to the many stores and restaurants found on and around the street that have Bukharian influences. Many Bukharians are also represented in parts of [Arizona](21883824.md), Miami, Florida, and areas of [Southern California](62520.md) such as San Diego.

There is a sizeable [Mountain Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_Jewish) population in [Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn), New York, that speaks [Judeo-Tat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judeo-Tat) (Juhuri), a dialect of Persian.[^cite_note-238]

[Classical Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Hebrew) is the language of most Jewish religious literature, such as the [Tanakh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanakh) (Bible) and [Siddur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siddur) (prayerbook). [Modern Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Hebrew) is also the primary official language of the modern State of [Israel](9282173.md), which further encourages many to learn it as a second language. Some recent Israeli immigrants to America speak Hebrew as their primary language.

There are a diversity of [Hispanic Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_Jews) living in America. The oldest community is that of the Sephardi Jews of New Netherland. Their ancestors had fled Spain or Portugal during the Inquisition for the Netherlands, and then came to New Netherland. Though there is dispute over whether they should be considered Hispanic. Some Hispanic Jews, particularly in Miami and Los Angeles, immigrated from Latin America. The largest groups are those that fled Cuba after the communist revolution (known as [Jewbans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewban)), Argentine Jews, and more recently, Venezuelan Jews. Argentina is the Latin American country with the largest Jewish population. There are a large number of synagogues in the Miami area that give services in Spanish. The last Hispanic Jewish community would be those that recently came from Portugal or Spain, after Spain and Portugal granted citizenship to the descendants of Jews who fled during the Inquisition. All the above listed Hispanic Jewish groups speak either Spanish or Ladino.

### Jewish American literature

Main article: [Jewish American literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_American_literature)

Although American Jews have contributed greatly to American arts in general, there still remains a distinctly Jewish American literature. Jewish American literature often explores the experience of being a Jew in America, and the conflicting pulls of secular society and history.

### Popular culture

Main articles: [Jews in American cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews_in_American_cinema), [Jews in baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews_in_baseball), [List of Jewish American authors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_American_authors), [List of Jewish American entertainers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_American_entertainers), [List of Jewish musicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_musicians), [List of Jewish American visual artists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_American_visual_artists), and [List of Jewish American photographers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_American_photographers)

Yiddish theater was very well attended, and provided a training ground for performers and producers who moved to Hollywood in the 1920s. Many of the early Hollywood moguls and pioneers were Jewish.[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240] They played roles in the development of radio and television networks, typified by [William S. Paley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_S._Paley) who ran [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS).[^cite_note-241] Stephen J. Whitfield states that "The Sarnoff family was long dominant at NBC."[^cite_note-242]

Many individual Jews have made significant contributions to American popular culture.[^cite_note-243] There have been many Jewish American actors and performers, ranging from early 1900s actors, to classic Hollywood film stars, and culminating in many currently known actors. The field of American comedy includes many Jews. The legacy also includes songwriters and authors, for example the author of the song "Viva Las Vegas" [Doc Pomus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doc_Pomus), or *[Billy the Kid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_the_Kid_\(ballet\))* composer [Aaron Copland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Copland). Many Jews have been at the forefront of women's issues.

### Sports

See also: [List of Jewish American sportspeople](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_American_sportspeople) and [Jews in baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews_in_baseball)



The rubber [spaldeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spaldeen), often used to play [variations of baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variations_of_baseball) in the street

[^cite_note-244]

The first generation of Jewish Americans who immigrated during the 1880–1924 peak period were not interested in [baseball](3850.md), the country's national pastime, and in some cases tried to prevent their children from watching or participating in baseball-related activities. Most were focused on making sure they and their children took advantage of education and employment opportunities. Despite the efforts of parents, Jewish children became interested in baseball quickly since it was already embedded in the broader American culture. The second generation of immigrants saw baseball as a means to celebrate American culture without abandoning their broader religious community. After 1924, many Yiddish newspapers began covering baseball, which they had not done previously.[^cite_note-245]

### Government and military

Main articles: [List of Jewish American politicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_American_politicians) and [List of Jewish Americans in the military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_Americans_in_the_military)

See also: [Military history of Jewish Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_Jewish_Americans)



Grave of a [Confederate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_Army) Jewish soldier near [Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinton,_Louisiana), [Louisiana](18130.md)

Since 1845, a total of 34 Jews have served in the Senate, including the 14 present-day senators noted [above](#Politics). [Judah P. Benjamin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judah_P._Benjamin) was the first practicing Jewish Senator, and would later serve as [Confederate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America) [Secretary of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_War) and Secretary of State during the [Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War). [Rahm Emanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahm_Emanuel) served as Chief of Staff to President Barack Obama. The number of Jews elected to the House rose to an all-time high of 30. [Eight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_United_States_Supreme_Court_justices) Jews have been appointed to the [United States Supreme Court](31737.md), of which one ([Elena Kagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elena_Kagan)) is currently serving. Had [Merrick Garland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merrick_Garland)'s 2016 nomination been accepted, that number would have risen to four out of nine since [Ruth Bader Ginsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Bader_Ginsburg) and [Stephen Breyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Breyer) were also serving at that time.

The Civil War marked a transition for American Jews. It killed off the antisemitic canard, widespread in Europe, to the effect that Jews are cowardly, preferring to run from war rather than serve alongside their fellow citizens in battle.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247]

At least twenty eight American Jews have been awarded the [Medal of Honor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medal_of_Honor).

### World War II

More than 550,000 Jews served in the [U.S. military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_the_United_States) during [World War II](32927.md); about 11,000 of them were killed and more than 40,000 of them were wounded. There were three recipients of the Medal of Honor; 157 recipients of the [Army Distinguished Service Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_Distinguished_Service_Medal), [Navy Distinguished Service Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navy_Distinguished_Service_Medal), [Distinguished Service Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distinguished_Service_Cross_\(United_States\)), or [Navy Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navy_Cross_\(United_States\)); and about 1600 recipients of the [Silver Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver_Star). About 50,000 other decorations and awards were given to Jewish military personnel, making a total of 52,000 decorations. During this period, Jews were approximately 3.3 percent of the total U.S. population but they constituted about 4.23 percent of the U.S. armed forces. About 60 percent of all Jewish physicians in the United States who were under 45 years of age were in service as military physicians and [medics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medic).[^cite_note-248]

Disproportionately many Jewish [physicists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicist), such as [Richard Feynman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Feynman), [Hans Bethe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Bethe), [John von Neumann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_von_Neumann) as well as the project head, [J. Robert Oppenheimer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Robert_Oppenheimer), were involved in the [Manhattan Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Project), the secret World War II effort to develop the [atomic bomb](21785.md).[^cite_note-249] Many of these physicists were refugees from [Nazi Germany](21212.md) or they were refugees from [antisemitic](1078.md) persecution which was also occurring elsewhere in Europe.

### American folk music

Jews have been involved in the American folk music scene since the late 19th century;[^cite_note-250] these tended to be refugees from Central and Eastern Europe, and significantly more economically disadvantaged than their established Western European Sephardic coreligionists.[^cite_note-251] Historians see it as a legacy of the secular Yiddish theater, cantorial traditions and a desire to assimilate. By the 1940s Jews had become established in the American folk music scene.

Examples of the major impact Jews have had in the American folk music arena include, but are not limited to: [Moe Asch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moe_Asch) the first to record and release much of the music of [Woody Guthrie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woody_Guthrie), including "[This Land is Your Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/This_Land_is_Your_Land)" (see [The Asch Recordings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Asch_Recordings)) in response to [Irving Berlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irving_Berlin)'s "God Bless America", and Guthrie wrote [Jewish songs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woody_Guthrie#Jewish_songs). Guthrie married a [Jew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_Guthrie) and their son [Arlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlo_Guthrie) became influential in his own right. Asch's one-man corporation Folkways Records also released much of the music of Leadbelly and Pete Seeger from the 1940s and 1950s. Asch's large music catalog was voluntarily donated to the [Smithsonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithsonian).

Jews have also [thrived in jazz music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Americans_in_Jazz) and contributed to its popularization.

Three of the four creators of the [Newport Folk Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newport_Folk_Festival), Wein, Bikel, and Grossman, were Jewish. Albert Grossman put together [Peter, Paul and Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter,_Paul_and_Mary), of which Yarrow is Jewish. [Oscar Brand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Brand), from a Canadian Jewish family, has the longest running radio program "Oscar Brand's Folksong Festival" which has been on air consecutively since 1945 from New York City.[^cite_note-252] And is the first American broadcast where the host himself will answer any personal correspondence.

The influential group [The Weavers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Weavers), successor to the Almanac Singers, led by Pete Seeger, had a Jewish manager, and two of the four members of the group were Jewish (Gilbert and Hellerman). The B-side of "Good Night Irene" had the Hebrew folk song personally chosen for the record by Pete Seeger "[Tzena, Tzena, Tzena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzena,_Tzena,_Tzena)".

The influential folk music magazine *Sing Out!* was co-founded and edited by [Irwin Silber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irwin_Silber) in 1951, and edited by him until 1967, when the magazine stopped publication for decades. *Rolling Stone* magazine's first music critic [Jon Landau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Landau) is of German Jewish descent. Izzy Young who created the legendary[^cite_note-253] Folklore Center in New York, and currently the Folklore Centrum near Mariatorget in Södermalm, Sweden, which relates to American and Swedish folk music.[^cite_note-254]

Dave Van Ronk observed that the behind the scenes 1950s folk scene "was at the very least 50 percent Jewish, and they adopted the music as part of their assimilation into the Anglo-American tradition which itself was largely an artificial construct but none the less provided us with some common ground".[^cite_note-255] Nobel Prize winner [Bob Dylan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Dylan) is also Jewish.

### Finance and law

Jews have been involved in financial services since the colonial era. They received rights to trade fur from the Dutch and Swedish colonies. British governors honored these rights after taking over. During the Revolutionary War, Haym Solomon helped create America's first semi-central bank, and advised Alexander Hamilton on the building of America's financial system.

American Jews in the 19th, 20th and 21st centuries played a major role in developing America's financial services industry, both at investment banks and with investment funds.[^cite_note-256] German Jewish bankers began to assume a major role in American finance in the 1830s when government and private borrowing to pay for canals, railroads and other [internal improvements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_improvements) increased rapidly and significantly. Men such as [August Belmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_Belmont) (Rothschild's agent in New York and a leading Democrat), Philip Speyer, [Jacob Schiff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Schiff) (at Kuhn, Loeb & Company), [Joseph Seligman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Seligman), [Philip Lehman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_Lehman) (of [Lehman Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lehman_Brothers)), [Jules Bache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Bache), and [Marcus Goldman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Goldman) (of [Goldman Sachs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goldman_Sachs)) illustrate this financial elite.[^cite_note-257] As was true of their non-Jewish counterparts, family, personal, and business connections, a reputation for honesty and integrity, ability, and a willingness to take calculated risks were essential to recruit capital from widely scattered sources. The families and the firms which they controlled were bound together by religious and social factors, and by the prevalence of intermarriage. These personal ties fulfilled real business functions before the advent of institutional organization in the 20th century.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259] Antisemitic elements often falsely targeted them as key players in a supposed Jewish cabal conspiring to dominate the world.[^cite_note-260]

Since the late 20th century, Jews have played a major role in the hedge fund industry, according to Zuckerman (2009).[^cite_note-261] Thus [SAC Capital Advisors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SAC_Capital_Advisors),[^cite_note-262] [Soros Fund Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soros_Fund_Management),[^cite_note-263] [Och-Ziff Capital Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Och-Ziff_Capital_Management),[^cite_note-264] [GLG Partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLG_Partners)[^cite_note-265] [Renaissance Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_Technologies)[^cite_note-266] and [Elliott Management Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliott_Management_Corporation)[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268] are large hedge funds cofounded by Jews. They have also played a pivotal role in the private equity industry, co-founding some of the largest firms in the United States, such as [Blackstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackstone_Group),[^cite_note-269] [Cerberus Capital Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerberus_Capital_Management),[^cite_note-270] [TPG Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TPG_Capital),[^cite_note-271] [BlackRock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BlackRock),[^cite_note-272] [Carlyle Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlyle_Group),[^cite_note-273] [Warburg Pincus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warburg_Pincus),[^cite_note-274] and [KKR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kohlberg_Kravis_Roberts).[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277]

Very few Jewish lawyers were hired by [White Anglo-Saxon Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Anglo-Saxon_Protestant) ("WASP") upscale [white-shoe law firms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-shoe_firm), but they started their own. The WASP dominance in law ended when a number of major Jewish law firms attained elite status in dealing with top-ranked corporations. As late as 1950 there was not a single large Jewish law firm in New York City. However, by 1965 six of the 20 largest firms were Jewish; by 1980 four of the ten largest were Jewish.[^cite_note-278]

#### Federal Reserve

[Paul Warburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Warburg), one of the leading advocates of the establishment of a [central bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_bank) in the United States and one of the first governors of the newly established [Federal Reserve System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_System), came from a prominent Jewish family in Germany.[^cite_note-279] Since then, several Jews have served as [chairmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman_of_the_Federal_Reserve) of the Fed, including [Eugene Meyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_Meyer_\(financier\)) (1930-1933), [Arthur F. Burns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_F._Burns) (1970-1978), [Alan Greenspan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Greenspan) (1987-2006), [Ben Bernanke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Bernanke) (2006-2014) and [Janet Yellen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janet_Yellen) (2014-2018).

### Science, business, and academia

Main articles: [List of Jewish American scientists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_American_scientists), [List of Jewish American businesspeople](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_American_businesspeople?action=edit\&redlink=1), and [List of Jewish American academics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jewish_American_academics)

> Jewish Americans have had a significant presence in entrepreneurship and various professional fields in the United States.[^cite_note-Frum-2010-130] Studies of Jewish American family businesses have examined how businesses passed between generations can provide continuing sources of income and family assets.[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283] Some scholars have identified entrepreneurship and participation in business and commerce as features of Jewish American economic life.[^cite_note-Nathan-Kazis-2014-284] American Jews have been active in various academic disciplines, including physics, sociology, economics, psychology, mathematics, philosophy and linguistics. Jewish American intellectuals such as Saul Bellow, Ayn Rand, Noam Chomsky, Thomas Friedman, Milton Friedman and Elie Wiesel have been prominent in American public and intellectual life. Of American Nobel Prize winners, 37 percent have been Jewish Americans, as have 61 percent of recipients of the John Bates Clark Medal in economic&#x73;**.**[^cite_note-285]

In the business world, it was found in 1995 that while Jewish Americans constituted less than 2.5 percent of the U.S. population, they occupied 7.7 percent of board seats at various U.S. [corporations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporations).[^cite_note-286] American Jews also have a strong presence in [NBA](22093.md) ownership. Of the 30 teams in the NBA, there are 14 Jewish principal owners. Several Jews have served as NBA commissioners including prior NBA commissioner [David Stern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Stern) and current commissioner [Adam Silver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Silver).[^cite_note-Nathan-Kazis-2014-284]

Since many careers in science, business, and academia generally pay well, Jewish Americans also tend to have a somewhat higher average income than most Americans. The 2000–2001 National Jewish Population Survey shows that the median income of a Jewish family is \$54,000 a year (\$5,000 more than the average family) and 34% of Jewish households report income over \$75,000 a year.[^cite_note-287]

### Food

Main article: [American Jewish cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Jewish_cuisine)

Jewish American people have had a large effect on the cuisine of the United States, with several [kosher-style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosher-style) delicatessens achieving mainstream popularity and defining American Jewish culture.[^cite_note-Savoring-288][^cite_note-289] For that reason, American Jewish food is typically associated with [Ashkenazi cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkenazi_cuisine), including foods such as [bagels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bagel), [knish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knishes), [gefilte fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gefilte_fish), [kreplach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kreplach), [matzoh ball soup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matzah_ball), [hamantash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamantash), [lox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lox), [kugel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kugel), [pastrami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pastrami), and [brisket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brisket). Other Jewish communities, such as the Sephardic community, have influenced the dishes served at American restaurants, particularly in New York City.[^cite_note-290]

A 2013 Pew Research Center analysis of Jewish identification showed that in addition to the 1.8% of US adults who identified their religion as Jewish (very similar to Gallup's estimate), another small percentage of Americans who did not initially say their religion was Jewish identified their secular heritage as Jewish. According to this research, at maximum 2.2% of the U.S. adult population has some basis for Jewish self-identification.

[^cite_note-82]: &#x20;Eyal, Gil (2006), ["The "One Million Plan" and the Development of a Discourse about the Absorption of the Jews from Arab Countries"](https://books.google.com/books?id=5d3iEECpQSoC\&pg=PA86), *The Disenchantment of the Orient: Expertise in Arab Affairs and the Israeli State*, Stanford University Press, pp. 86–90, [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [9780804754033](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/9780804754033) p. 86: "The principal significance of this plan lies in the fact, noted by [Yehuda Shenhav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yehuda_Shenhav), that this was the first time in Zionist history that Jews from Middle Eastern and North African countries were all packaged together in one category as the target of an immigration plan. There were earlier plans to bring specific groups, such as the Yemenites, but the "one million plan" was, as Shenhav says, "the zero point," the moment when the category of Mizrahi Jews in the current sense of this term, as an ethnic group distinct from European-born Jews, was invented." p.89—90 ..."Shehav's argument that the one million plan led to the invention of the category of Mizrahi Jews and gave the term the meaning it has today, because the plan treated all Jews who originated in these countries as belonging to a single category of candidates for immigration. ... it added another layer of meaning to the newly minted and still crystallizing Mizrahi category, that is, as implying a quasi-racial division between those who had an "oriental appearance" and those who did not."

Jewish Women's Archive. Accessed December 12, 2015. "Today, many American Jews retain an ambivalence about whiteness, despite the fact that the vast majority have benefited and continue to benefit from white privilege. This ambivalence stems from many different places: a deep connection to a Jewish history of discrimination and otherness; a moral imperative to identify with the stranger; an anti-universalist impulse that does not want Jews to be among the "melted" in the proverbial melting pot; an experience of prejudice and awareness of the contingency of whiteness; a feeling that Jewish identity is not fully described by religion but has some ethnic/tribal component that feels more accurately described by race; and a discomfort with contemporary Jewish power and privilege."

["American Jews, Race, Identity, and the Civil Rights Movement"](https://web.archive.org/web/20170714211748/https://jwa.org/teach/livingthelegacy/american-jews-race-identity-and-civil-rights-movement). *Jewish Women's Archive*. Archived from [the original](http://jwa.org/teach/livingthelegacy/american-jews-race-identity-and-civil-rights-movement) on July 14, 2017. Retrieved December 12, 2015. Today, many American Jews retain an ambivalence about whiteness, despite the fact that the vast majority have benefited and continue to benefit from white privilege. This ambivalence stems from many different places: a deep connection to a Jewish history of discrimination and otherness; a moral imperative to identify with the stranger; an anti-universalist impulse that does not want Jews to be among the "melted" in the proverbial melting pot; an experience of prejudice and awareness of the contingency of whiteness; a feeling that Jewish identity is not fully described by religion but has some ethnic/tribal component that feels more accurately described by race; and a discomfort with contemporary Jewish power and privilege.

[^cite_note-Seliktar-2007-226]: &#x20;Seliktar, Ofira (2007). ["The Changing Identity of American Jews, Israel and the Peace Process"](https://books.google.com/books?id=1bXETKMOI1cC\&pg=PA126). In Ben-Moshe, Danny; Segev, Zohar (eds.). [*Israel, the Diaspora, and Jewish Identity*](https://archive.org/details/israeldiasporaje0000unse/page/126). Sussex Academic Press. p. [126](https://archive.org/details/israeldiasporaje0000unse/page/126). [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-1-84519-189-4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-1-84519-189-4). Retrieved January 20, 2016. The 1993 Oslo Agreement made this split in the Jewish community official. Prime Minister Yitzak Rabin's handshake with Yasir Arafat during the September 13 White House ceremony elicited dramatically opposed reactions among American Jews. To the liberal universalists the accord was highly welcome news. As one commentator put it, after a year of tension between Israel and the United States, "there was an audible sigh of relief from American and Jewish liberals. Once again, they could support Israel as good Jews, committed liberals, and loyal Americans." The community "could embrace the Jewish state, without compromising either its liberalism or its patriotism". \
    However, to some right wing Jews, the peace treaty was worrisome. From their perspective, Oslo was not just an affront to the sanctity of how they interpreted their culture, but also a personal threat to the lives and livelihood in the West Bank and Gaza, territory which was historically known as "Judea and Samaria". For these Jews, such as Morton Klein, the president of the Zionist organization of America, and Norman Podhoretz, the editor of *Commentary*, the peace treaty amounted to an appeasement of Palestinian terrorism. They and others repeatedly warned that the newly established Palestinian Authority (PA) would pose a serious security threat to Israel.

[^cite_note-227]: &#x20;Lasensky, Scott (March 2002). Rubin, Barry (ed.). ["Underwriting Peace in the Middle East: U.S. Foreign Policy and the Limits of Economic Inducements"](https://web.archive.org/web/20090510071636/http://meria.idc.ac.il/journal/2002/issue1/jv6n1a7.html). *Middle East Review of International Affairs*. **6** (1). Archived from [the original](http://meria.idc.ac.il/journal/2002/issue1/jv6n1a7.html) on May 10, 2009. The Palestinian aid effort was certainly not helped by the heated debate that quickly developed inside the Beltway. Not only was the Israeli electorate divided on the Oslo accords, but so, too, was the American Jewish community, particularly at the leadership level and among the major New York and Washington-based public interest groups. American Jews opposed to Oslo joined Israelis "who brought their domestic issues to Washington" and together they pursued a campaign that focused most of its attention on Congress and the aid program. The dynamic was new to Washington. The Administration, the Rabin-Peres government, and some American Jewish groups teamed on one side while Israeli opposition groups and anti-Oslo American Jewish organizations pulled Congress in the other direction.

[^cite_note-Savoring-288]: &#x20;Smith, Andrew F.; Oliver, Garrett (2015). [*Savoring Gotham: A Food Lover's Companion to New York City*](https://books.google.com/books?id=KBGnCgAAQBAJ). Oxford University Press. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [9780190263645](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/9780190263645). Some Jewish delicatessen served only kosher meat, but many were kosher style, meaning that they served meat not certified as kosher but did not serve dairy (to avoid mixing with meat) or any pork and shellfish products. With Jewish acculturation to the mainstream, however, the concept of kosher-style delicatessens broadened to include smoked meat sandwiches with slices of cheese or luscious New York-style cheesecakes for dessert - if not the occasional ham sandwich.
